# CEREBRO Patch Registry

Permanent inline modifications and fork-additions in upstream-zone files. Each entry
documents one named patch + its rationale + the file location(s).

**Marker format:** `// CEREBRO-PATCH(<name>): <description>` (or language-appropriate
comment for SQL/CSS/SBPL/JSON-with-_comment-field).

## Summary

- **Unique patch names:** 297 (273 baseline + 15 PR #118 markers + 6 JEH-725 markers + 1 JEH-1360 marker + 2 TECH-3100 terminal bug fixes)
- **Files marked (including chunks 4-8 pre-existing markers):** 302
- **Files newly marked in chunk 11:** 280 (chunk-11 scope)
- **Total marker lines added in chunk 11:** ~280 (one comment line per file)
- **Marker lines added in PR #118:** 16 (managed Firtal Gateway runtime scope)
- **Total fork-vs-upstream `+` lines (chunk-11 scope, across 280 files):** 22,170
- **Phase 4 target for cerebro-mod lines:** ≤200 (escalation threshold: >300)

> **Status: ESCALATED.** The 22,170 `+`-line count is far above the original Phase 4 target of ≤200.
> The original audit estimated 42 L3-marked files; reality includes ~280 upstream-zone files,
> the majority of which are net-new fork features (artifacts, profile, MCP, inbox-folders,
> work-sessions, project-access, budgets) sitting in upstream paths rather than `cerebro-*` packages.
> True L3 inline-modifications (where cerebro logic is woven into upstream files) are a small
> subset; the rest should ideally be RELOCATED to `cerebro-*` packages in a follow-up phase.
> See `Categories` section below.

## Categories

| Category | File count | Lines (`+`) | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| Net-new fork files in upstream paths | 126 | 17917 | Marker on file header; candidate for relocation to `cerebro-*` |
| True L3 inline modifications | 154 | 4253 | Marker on file header (or inline at modification site for chunks 4-8) |

| `strip-md-bold-italic-trailing` | packages/ui/markdown/linkify.ts | 8 | TECH-2952 — linkify-it does not recognize `**` (double-asterisk) as a URL boundary, so `**https://url.com**` was detected as `https://url.com**` with the trailing `**` included in the link text and href. Agents routinely bold-format URLs this way. The fix strips any trailing asterisks from the detected URL in `collectLinkifyMatches`; the stripped `**` remain in the surrounding text, so react-markdown correctly renders them as a bold link. Single `*` already worked (linkify-it handles it); only `**` and longer runs are affected. |

| `orchestration-stalled-handoff` | server/internal/handler/orchestration_watchdog_cerebro.go<br>server/internal/handler/orchestration_cerebro.go<br>server/internal/handler/orchestration_cerebro_test.go | ~110 | FIR-2687 — stalled-worker takeover. Previously the watchdog re-dispatched a stalled sub-issue once, then gave up and froze the flow waiting for a human (the failure Jesper hit: an agent ran out of usage mid-task and the whole orchestration never reached the goal). Now the recovery ladder is bounded re-dispatch → hand-off → human: on the second stall the watchdog hands the sub-issue to the squad leader (a backup builder, via `handStalledChildToLeader`) to take over and finish, marks it `orch-handed-off`, and only escalates to a human if it is STILL stalled after that. Markers cleared by `clearStallMarkers` when the child recovers/terminates so a future stall re-runs the full ladder. |

All logic lives in the cerebro-zone file `server/internal/handler/orchestration_cerebro.go` + `server/internal/cerebro/orchestration/subissue.go`. The label is the only control (per-issue opt-in; remove it to stop) — there is no workspace flag. Sub-issues are driven primarily via squads (squad leader woken), with agent assignees also supported. |

Previously `selectVerifierAgent` only ever picked the squad leader; when the leader was also the agent that built the sub-issue (the common small-squad case where the leader assigns work to herself) it returned "no verifier" and fell back to the human label-gate, stranding the run on an `orch-verified` label nobody added. Now, when the leader is the worker, the engine falls back to an independent squad member (`selectIndependentSquadMember`), preferring a reviewer/QA-role member, before the human gate. Only if no independent agent member exists does it ask a human. |

| `child-done-notify-flag` | server/internal/handler/issue_child_done.go (3-line gate)<br>server/internal/handler/issue.go (2 call-site args)<br>server/internal/handler/github.go (1 call-site arg)<br>server/internal/handler/issue_child_notify_flag_cerebro.go (new)<br>server/internal/handler/issue_child_status_cerebro.go (per-status gate)<br>packages/cerebro-feature-flags/registry.ts (3 flags)<br>packages/cerebro-feature-flags/settings-tab.tsx (workspace 3-way control) | 6 inline + new file + 3 TS flags + UI | TECH-3006 — adds three default-ON feature flags, one per child→parent notification status: `cerebro_child_done_notify_parent` (done), `cerebro_child_in_review_notify_parent` (in_review), `cerebro_child_blocked_notify_parent` (blocked). Each status can be silenced independently. Admin can set each to Members-choose / Forced-on / Forced-off for the whole workspace via Settings → Cerebro Flags (the Forced-off control is the reliable org-wide kill switch — a locked workspace-off suppresses the notification regardless of which actor flips the sub-issue); members may set a personal override when the workspace value is not locked. Flag-checking logic lives in the new `issue_child_notify_flag_cerebro.go` and mirrors registry precedence: locked workspace > personal > unlocked workspace > default ON. `issue_child_done.go` carries a marked gate at the top of `notifyParentOfChildDone`; `issue_child_status_cerebro.go` gates `in_review`/`blocked` on their own per-status flags; `issue.go` passes the request user so personal overrides apply, while GitHub automation falls back to the workspace/default value. |

| `auto-pause-on-failure` | server/internal/service/task.go | 12 | JEH-1035 / FIR-1992 — `FailTask` runs `AutoPause.MaybeAutoPauseOnFailure` BEFORE auto-retry so a paused runtime blocks new retry creation; `autoPaused` also gates the issue comment, chat failure message, and quick-create notification so rate-limit failures do not spam on every 5-min fail-safe loop tick. |
| `retry-pause-guard` | server/internal/service/task.go | ~20 | FIR-2442 — `MaybeRetryFailedTask` skips retry creation when the parent task's runtime is paused. Without this, the orphan-recovery / `HandleFailedTasks` path keeps minting retry rows on a paused runtime — `ClaimTask` rejects each one immediately with `runtime_paused`, so no LLM is called and no spend occurs, but the queue (and the UI's "retries being attempted" surface) fills with dead rows. Tine's runtime accumulated 299 such rows in a single rate-limit window. Lookup error is fail-safe: skip retry rather than risk a runaway on unknown pause state. Complements `auto-pause-on-failure` which only fires on the synchronous `FailTask` path; this guard covers the orphan / sweeper path that calls `MaybeRetryFailedTask` directly. |
| `suppress-serverside-timeout-blocked` | server/internal/service/task.go<br>server/internal/service/task_timeout_comment_test.go | 3 inline + test | FIR-2609 — the serverside stale-task sweeper (`FailStaleTasks`) sets `error="task timed out"` with `failure_reason=timeout` for two situations folded into one string: a still-`dispatched` task a paused/offline/busy runtime never started, and a `running` task that started but stalled. Only the first is bookkeeping noise (the runtime was away — the agent never ran), and both timeout-comment firing paths (`FailTask` + `HandleFailedTasks`) posted a scary "BLOCKED: Agent-run timed out" comment for it (the dispatched/never-started case is the dominant share of the 703 "task timed out" errors / 16.3% of all real errors — the primary user-reported noise in FIR-2340). `isUnstartedServersideTimeout(task, errMsg)` keys on a missing `started_at` to gate both `createAgentComment` calls: a never-started dispatch-timeout is now silent (run-log only), while a genuinely started-then-stalled run still surfaces BLOCKED (preserving FIR-2395 Case C `TestTimeoutPostsBlockedCommentForTrulySilentHang`), as do agent-internal timeouts ("`<agent> timed out after <dur>`") — all subject to the FIR-2395 already-replied gate. |

| `daemon-per-tool-call-timeout` | server/internal/daemon/config.go<br>server/internal/daemon/config_test.go<br>server/internal/daemon/daemon.go<br>server/internal/daemon/daemon_test.go | ~140 | FIR-2610 — per-tool-call hard cap (`MaxToolCallDuration`, env `MULTICA_MAX_TOOL_CALL_DURATION`, default 30m, 0=disable). The idle watchdog deliberately stands down while a tool call is in-flight (a long build emits no messages), so a tool call that hangs forever (a `sleep`/`until` poll loop, a frozen network call, an unresponsive MCP server) sat at "running" until the coarse 2h `DefaultAgentTimeout`. This cap force-stops it in minutes via a dedicated `tool_timeout` disposition ("tool call exceeded MaxToolCallDuration"). **Not cerebro-specific — a genuine upstream gap; fork-carried ONLY until upstream PR multica-ai/multica#3574 merges, then dropped on the next sync.** Exceeds the 5-line guideline because the cap is woven into the existing watchdog goroutine / drain loop and cannot be cleanly isolated into a cerebro-zone module without diverging from the upstream form it's meant to become; kept inline per Jesper's direction on FIR-2610 (approver: Jesper Hvejsel, 2026-05-31). |

| `chat-bubble-wrap-long-words` | packages/views/chat/components/chat-message-list.tsx | 5 | FIR-2560 — long URLs / unbreakable tokens in user and assistant chat content forced horizontal scroll on narrow chat panels (Mia floating chat on mobile width). Fix is two-part: (1) `min-w-0` on the user bubble's outer flex wrapper so the flex item can shrink below intrinsic content width and actually respect `max-w-[80%]`; (2) `wrap-anywhere` (overflow-wrap: anywhere) on the bubble + every assistant prose container so a long URL lowers min-content and breaks inside the column instead of pushing layout wider than the viewport. `break-words` (overflow-wrap: break-word) alone was insufficient because it does not adjust min-content sizing on flex children. |
| `channels-mobile-col-min-width` | packages/views/channels/components/channel-detail.tsx | 2 | TECH-3490 — the DM/Channel message column (`relative flex flex-1 min-h-0 flex-col`) had no `min-w-0`, so on mobile it could not shrink below its content's intrinsic width and rendered 434px wide inside a 390px viewport — clipping every message line at the right edge (Jesper's Jakob DM screenshot). The earlier markdown-overflow-guard / editor overflow-wrap rules never applied here because channel messages render through `ReadonlyContent` (`.rich-text-editor`), a different surface than `.markdown-content`. Verified live at 390px: column 434→390, document overflow count many→0. Adds `min-w-0`. |
| `markdown-overflow-guard` | packages/ui/markdown/markdown.css | 2 | TECH-3490 — completes FIR-2560. `wrap-anywhere` was placed on the chat bubble/prose containers, but `.markdown-content` itself carries the `break-words` utility (overflow-wrap: break-word), which the actual text nodes (`p`/`a`/`code`) inherit — and break-word does NOT lower min-content. Verified live at 390px: the inbox chat scroll container measured scrollWidth 471 vs clientWidth 390 (81px horizontal overflow), user bubble 381px vs its 280px cap; after the rule, 0px overflow and bubble 280px. Sets `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.markdown-content` + its text descendants so long URLs/tokens break at the source on every markdown surface (chat, comments, descriptions, skills), not just chat. Unlayered import wins over the Tailwind utility layer. |

New `GET /api/issues/{id}/context` returns issue + recent thread + members + labels in ONE call, exposed as `multica issue context <id>`. When the saving is "on" the claim handler sets `BundleContextHint` and the start prompt points the agent at the single bundled call; the real save is measured at the endpoint per task (counts actual usage, not an assumed save). "shadow" records the would-save at claim time without changing behaviour; snapshot_prompt takes precedence so the two never double-count. Net-new logic + measurement live in cerebro-zone files (server/internal/handler/issue_context_cerebro.go, server/internal/handler/daemon_cost_bundled_cerebro.go, server/internal/daemon/cost_bundled_cerebro.go); these are the irreducible upstream wiring edits (route, CLI command, Task/response field, prompt guards, claim-handler call). |

Two `TaskContextForEnv` flags (`IssueSnapshotInlined`, `BundleContextHint`) carry the active saving into the brief; the read steps now point at the inlined snapshot (snapshot) or the single `multica issue context` call (bundled). snapshot_prompt has precedence. | `inbox-pin-selected` | packages/views/inbox/components/inbox-page.tsx | 4 small touchpoints | FIR-2474 — keeps the open inbox row anchored in place: its sort time is frozen at the moment it is opened, so incoming activity for that row no longer reorders it under the reader. The row re-sorts to its natural position only after the message is closed (selection cleared). Logic lives in `@multica/cerebro-inbox` (`pin-selected.ts`); the upstream `mergedEntries` sort delegates to `sortInboxEntriesPinned` and adds `selectedKey` to its deps. |
| `inbox-pin-selected-group` | packages/views/inbox/components/inbox-page.tsx | 5 small touchpoints | FIR-2474 (follow-up) — extends the pin to the GROUPED views. The original `inbox-pin-selected` only froze the open row's sort *time*, which holds it in place in the flat time-sorted list but not when the inbox is grouped (Group by → Action / Project / Agent / Type — Action is the default). When grouped, every row is bucketed live from signals that change with activity (run state, mentions, read/unread), so incoming activity moved the open row to a different group. This patch freezes the open row's bucket per group-mode at open time so it stays in the same group until closed (or until the user deliberately changes the grouping). Logic lives in `@multica/cerebro-inbox` (`pin-selected-group.ts`, `pinnedBucketizer`); the upstream `groupedEntries` memo delegates both its primary and secondary `bucketize` calls through it and adds `selectedKey` to its deps. |
| `inbox-action-grouping` | packages/views/inbox/components/inbox-page.tsx | ~11 small touchpoints | FIR-2115 — adds a "Group by → Action" inbox grouping that buckets entries by what to do next: **Unread** (any unread inbox item), **Running** (agent currently running on the issue/chat), **Done** (terminal issue status: done/cancelled, or read non-issue chat/channel), **Waiting** (open issue thread you've already seen, but which is not running or done yet). Buckets render in fixed precedence order (not alphabetical) via an optional `order` on `Group`, with Waiting last. Default grouping for new users, gated by `cerebro_inbox_action_grouping` (snaps back to "none" when disabled). All classification, labels, and ordering live in `@multica/cerebro-inbox` (`action-groups.ts` + `strings.ts`); the upstream page only delegates via `bucketizeInboxAction` / `useInboxActionGroupLabels` / `INBOX_ACTION_GROUP_BY_OPTION`. |

Routed through Tiptap's `autofocus` option (not a post-mount RAF) because `immediatelyRender: false` defers editor creation past any effect that calls `editor.commands.focus()` — the call no-ops because `editor` is `null`. ContentEditor checks `document.activeElement` once at mount and yields silently if a `[role="dialog"]` already owns focus. Parents (`ChatInput`, `CommentInput`) gate the prop on `disabled` / `noAgent` and re-key the editor on session/agent/channel switch so Tiptap re-applies autofocus for the new context. |

| `issue-mention-new-tab` | packages/views/editor/readonly-content.tsx | 28 | JEH-874 + JEH-1048 + JEH-1112 — clicking an issue mention chip in a comment or description opens it in a new tab on desktop/wide-web via `openInNewTab(path)` / `window.open(path)`, with `href={path}` (relative). Earlier revisions used `getShareableUrl(path)` so right-click → Copy Link returned a full https:// URL; on cerebro deployments the desktop `appUrl` default (`https://multica.ai` in `apps/desktop/src/shared/runtime-config.ts`) made every chip visibly point at multica.ai instead of the host the app is actually served from, so the relative path is preferable. Copy-Link now returns a relative path. On mobile (`useIsMobile()` true) the chip falls back to SPA `push(path)` because the PWA renders in `display: standalone` (no tab UI) and `target="_blank"` either navigates the current window (iOS) or breaks out into the system browser (Android), both of which lose the thread context. Browser-back restores scroll + tiptap draft via the Next.js router cache. |

| `task-token-drop-fork-059` | server/migrations/108_task_token.up.sql | 4 | 2026-05-27 upstream sync — the fork's own per-task token (JEH-324, migration 059, `token_hash BYTEA` key) is superseded by upstream's MUL-2600 model (migration 108, `id`+`token_hash TEXT`+`user_id`). Both create a `task_token` table, so 108's bare `CREATE TABLE` would fail where 059 already ran (prod). A `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS task_token;` ahead of 108's CREATE recreates it in upstream's shape; safe because task tokens are short-lived. Approved by Jesper (FIR-2346). |

| `cerebro-exchange-rates-ingest-route` | server/cmd/server/router.go | 3 | FIR-43 — import + handler instance + public key-gated route `POST /api/cerebro/exchange-rates`. Service-to-service ingestion from `multica-hatchet-worker`: the worker fetches USD→{DKK,EUR} from Frankfurter and POSTs the snapshot here (Bearer `CEREBRO_EXCHANGE_INGEST_KEY`, loopback-only when unset). Backend is the sole writer of `cerebro_exchange_rates`, so the worker holds no `DATABASE_URL`. Handler + client live in the cerebro-zone `internal/cerebro/exchangerates` package (no marker needed); reuses the existing `Store` + queries (no sqlc regen). |
| `cerebro-github-pr-link` + `cerebro-github-pr-link-route` | server/internal/handler/github_pr_link_cerebro.go (new)<br>server/cmd/server/router.go | new file + 4 | FIR-2568 — poll-based PR↔issue linking. firtal-data-registry's deploy-scan already polls GitHub via the installed App; this adds an inbound service endpoint `POST /api/cerebro/github/pull-requests` (Bearer `CEREBRO_GITHUB_LINK_KEY`, loopback-only when unset; default workspace from `CEREBRO_GITHUB_LINK_WORKSPACE_SLUG`) that runs the SAME upsert + `extractIdentifiers` + `LinkIssueToPullRequest` logic as the webhook (`handlePullRequestEvent`), writing into `issue_pull_request` so the PR sidebar populates without a webhook/secret. The net-new logic lives entirely in the cerebro-zone file (reuses the package's unexported helpers); router.go change is the 2 marked handler-instance + route lines. No sqlc regen (reuses existing queries). |

| `reconnect-timeline-invalidate` | packages/core/realtime/use-realtime-sync.ts | 2 | FIR-1941 — the WS reconnect sweep only invalidates workspace-scoped query keys (`["issues", wsId, ...]`, inbox, etc.), but the per-issue timeline cache is keyed `["issues", "timeline", issueId]` and was never refreshed. A `comment:created` event dropped during an offline window left the timeline marked "fresh" (default `staleTime: Infinity`), so clicking the inbox notification opened the issue without the new comment. The patch invalidates the `["issues", "timeline"]` prefix inside `invalidateWorkspaceScopedQueries`, so reconnect and workspace-switch both mark all timelines stale; the active observer refetches immediately and inactive ones refetch on next mount. |

Two patch lines: one import (`cerebroreferences`) and one handler instantiation; two route blocks. Handler lives in `server/internal/cerebro/references/`. |

Three patch lines: one import (`cerebrocomments`), one handler instantiation, and one route registration inside the existing `/api/comments/{commentId}` block. | `cerebro-orchestration` | server/cmd/multica/main.go | 2 | FIR-2564 — register the `multica orchestrate` command (GroupID + AddCommand). Computes execution waves (Kahn topo-sort), rejects cycles/missing/self deps before running, runs ready nodes up to a concurrency limit with a single-writer lock (a `write` node runs exclusively), blocks downstream on failure, and retries per node. `run` executes a local simulation (--dry-run) today; wiring `executeNode` to live agent dispatch / sub-issue promotion is the gated server-side step. `orchestrate run --issue <id>` is label-gated on `orchestrate` so the engine stays inert on every issue until a human attaches the label — that is the early-rollout safety guarantee, not a feature flag. |

| `skill-skillmd-collision-guard` | server/internal/daemon/execenv/context.go | 5 | A Multica skill stores its `SKILL.md` body in the `content` field; `writeSkillFiles` writes that to `<dir>/SKILL.md`, then iterates the bundled supporting files. Real cases: the `prd-runbook` and `skills-runbook` workspace skills. The guard skips any bundled file whose cleaned path is `SKILL.md`, since `content` already owns that path. Net-new cerebro table `cerebro_comment_task(comment_id PK, task_id, issue_id, workspace_id)` keeps the upstream `comment` row untouched while still pinning each agent comment to the task that produced it (the `X-Task-ID` the CLI already stamps). New endpoint `GET /api/issues/{id}/comment-costs` returns each task's spend keyed by the **last** comment that task produced, so a run posting progress + result places the badge on the final comment — no double-counting and the per-comment numbers sum to the existing issue total in the sidebar (JEH-736 / IssueUsageSummary). The `cerebro_comment_cost` flag (default ON) gates UI + endpoint. The badge component lives in `cerebro-channels` (channels reuse the comment table, so one query backs both surfaces); upstream touch points are only the route line, response type, api-client method, the query key under `issueKeys.usage()` so the existing `["issues", "usage"]` task-lifecycle invalidation refreshes it, plus the badge/chip mounts in comment-card / slack-message-view / thread-side-panel / channel-detail. Channels also get an accumulated channel-total chip in the header (mirror of FIR-31's SessionCostChip) wired to the existing `/api/issues/{id}/usage` endpoint. |

| `daemon-ws-write-accessor-signal-order` | server/internal/daemon/wakeup.go | 2 | TECH-3100 — `signalTaskWakeup` was called before `d.wsWrites.Store(&writes)`. | `daemon-cerebro-term-buffer` | server/internal/daemon/daemon.go<br>server/internal/daemon/cerebro_terminal.go<br>server/internal/daemon/wakeup.go | ~95 | TECH-3388 — the live terminal rides only the daemonws WS, which Cloudflare flaps every ~30-90 min (`close 1001 "CloudFlare WebSocket proxy restarting"`). Before, `cerebroTermPump.flush` dropped every `term_stdout` frame while the WS was down (`term_stdout dropped: ws not connected`), so output during the ~30s gap was lost and the terminal went blank. Fix: a per-task ordered backlog (`cerebroTermBuffers`) keeps the output; `sendOrBufferTermStdout` buffers when the WS can't send (preserving order behind any pending backlog), and the WS reconnect path in `wakeup.go` calls `drainAllCerebroTermBuffers` to replay each task's backlog in order after re-emitting the attach frames. Bounded at `cerebroTermBufferMaxBytes` (512 KB) per task, oldest-dropped-first. Does NOT add an HTTP fallback — it survives flaps, not a permanently-down WS. |
| `daemonws-term-read-limit` | server/internal/daemonws/hub.go | 1 | TECH-3100 — `SetReadLimit(4096)` was sized for heartbeat frames only. `cerebro:term_stdout` frames are base64-encoded at the `cerebroTermFlushBytes = 8 KB` ceiling, producing ~11 KB frames. gorilla/websocket closed the daemonws connection on the first large flush, preventing any terminal output from reaching the browser. Fix: raise to `128 * 1024`. |

| `editor-mobile-table-wrap` | packages/views/editor/styles/cerebro-overrides.css | 4 inline (in a `@media (max-width:640px)` block) | JEH — email/message bodies pack prose into fixed-width layout tables; on a phone there is no room to scroll one sideways, so the row reads as "text cut off the right edge" and the user thinks text disappears. Below the mobile breakpoint, tables get `table-layout: fixed; width: 100%; min-width: 0; max-width: 100%` and their cells `white-space: normal; overflow-wrap: anywhere; width: auto` — all `!important` so they beat the **inline** `width="600"`/`style="width:680px"` that email clients emit. The table fits the column and wraps instead of forcing horizontal scroll. Desktop keeps the `min-width: max-content` + `white-space: nowrap` scroll behaviour from `editor-mobile-table-overflow`. Verified on WebKit (iPhone engine) at 390px: a 1212px-wide layout table collapses to 332px and reads fully. |

Re-export both from `inbox-list-item.tsx` — an already-cerebro-patched file whose subpath (`./inbox/components/inbox-list-item`) the dynamic package already imports — instead of widening the exports map. Pure re-export, no behaviour change for the classic inbox. |

| `delegation-latest-human-fallback` | server/internal/service/task.go<br>server/pkg/db/queries/comment.sql<br>server/pkg/db/generated/comment.sql.go | ~10 inline + new query | Delegation gate (JEH-1436) blocked an agent from mentioning another agent whenever its task carried no `original_user_id` and the issue creator was not a member — e.g. an agent replying to a human on an issue the agent itself created (observed: Mia could not start Sara after Jesper commented). `CommentDelegationContext` now adds a final fallback: inherit the most recent human (`author_type='member'`) comment on the issue as the delegation origin, via new query `GetLatestMemberCommentAuthor`. Pairs with the cerebro-zone wakeup change (`wakeup/service.go resolveWakeupOrigin`) that seeds the same human onto wakeup-dispatched runs. |
| `daemon-connection-tool-ask` | server/internal/handler/daemon_tool_policy_cerebro.go | ~25 inline | TECH-3498 — route **Ask** on a workspace-connection MCP tool through the existing approval inbox on the Claude-daemon engine. The generic `tools:<name>` resolve in the daemon tool-policy endpoint never matches the connection chain (`PolicyToolKey` keeps the `mcp__` name as-is), so a connection-tool Ask/Deny was invisible to the local-runtime resolve. The patch folds `toolpolicy.Store.ConnectionToolEffective` (the new Ask-capable generalisation of `ConnectionToolDenied`, in the cerebro zone) into the resolve result by TIGHTENING, so a connection-tool Ask drives the same PreToolUse inbox + long-poll path as any other Ask, and a connection-tool Deny blocks at runtime too. Fail-closed under enforce / keep-base under observe on a connection-resolve error, matching the existing resource-pattern fold. Gateway half (`runtime/approval_gate.go`) is fully in the cerebro zone, so no marker there. |
